_ test_failed_shared_mutation_cannot_erase_a_successful_competitor[none-book] __

tmp_path = PosixPath('/tmp/pytest-of-agent/pytest-1/test_failed_shared_mutation_ca0')
kind = 'book', fault = 'none'

    @pytest.mark.parametrize("kind", ["book", "journal"])
    @pytest.mark.parametrize("fault", ["none", "before_write", "after_write"])
    def test_failed_shared_mutation_cannot_erase_a_successful_competitor(tmp_path, kind, fault):
        path = tmp_path / "state.json"
        value = create(kind, path)
        entered, release, second_started, second_done = (threading.Event() for _ in range(4))
        target = "quant.book.ledger.write_json" if kind == "book" else "quant.desk.journal.write_json"
    
        def publication(path, document):
            if threading.current_thread().name == "first-accounting-writer":
                entered.set()
                assert release.wait(timeout=5)
                if fault == "before_write":
                    raise OSError("first write failed")
                write_json(path, document)
                if fault == "after_write":
                    raise OSError("first ACK lost")
            else:
                write_json(path, document)
    
        def first():
            threading.current_thread().name = "first-accounting-writer"
            try:
                mutate(kind, value, "first")
                return "written"
            except OSError:
                return "failed"
    
        def second():
            second_started.set()
            try:
                mutate(kind, value, "second")
                return "written"
            finally:
                second_done.set()
    
        with patch(target, side_effect=publication):
            with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2) as pool:
                a = pool.submit(first)
                assert entered.wait(timeout=5)
                b = pool.submit(second)
                try:
                    assert second_started.wait(timeout=5)
>                   assert not second_done.wait(timeout=0.15)
E                   assert not True

    @pytest.mark.parametrize("kind", ["book", "journal"])
    @pytest.mark.parametrize("target", ["os.open", "fcntl.flock", "pathlib.Path.mkdir"])
    def test_accounting_lock_setup_failure_restores_mutation_before_retry(tmp_path, kind, target):
        path = tmp_path / "state.json"
        value = create(kind, path)
        before = path.read_bytes()
        with patch(target, side_effect=OSError("lock setup failed")):
            with pytest.raises(OSError):
                mutate(kind, value, "retry")

_ test_identity_query_waits_until_mutation_is_durable_or_rolled_back[False-book] _

tmp_path = PosixPath('/tmp/pytest-of-agent/pytest-1/test_identity_query_waits_unti0')
kind = 'book', failure = False

    @pytest.mark.parametrize("kind", ["book", "journal"])
    @pytest.mark.parametrize("failure", [False, True])
    def test_identity_query_waits_until_mutation_is_durable_or_rolled_back(tmp_path, kind, failure):
        path = tmp_path / "state.json"
        value = create(kind, path)
        entered, release, reader_started, reader_done = (threading.Event() for _ in range(4))
        target = "quant.book.ledger.write_json" if kind == "book" else "quant.desk.journal.write_json"
    
        def publication(path, document):
            entered.set()
            assert release.wait(timeout=5)
            if failure:
                raise OSError("write failed")
            write_json(path, document)
    
        def write():
            try:
                mutate(kind, value, "one")
            except OSError:
                return "failed"
            return "written"
    
        def read():
            reader_started.set()
            try:
                return seen(kind, value, "one")
            finally:
                reader_done.set()
    
        with patch(target, side_effect=publication):
            with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2) as pool:
                writer = pool.submit(write)
                assert entered.wait(timeout=5)
                reader = pool.submit(read)
                try:
                    assert reader_started.wait(timeout=5)
>                   assert not reader_done.wait(timeout=0.15)
E                   assert not True
E                    +  where True = wait(timeout=0.15)
E                    +    where wait = <threading.Event at 0x7f901ff5e8a0: set>.wait

tmp_path = PosixPath('/tmp/pytest-of-agent/pytest-1/test_competing_stale_writers_c0')

    def test_competing_stale_writers_cannot_silently_erase_a_fill(tmp_path):
        path = tmp_path / "book.json"
        books = [Ledger(path, authority="EVALUATION", initial_capital=100), Ledger(path)]
        gate = threading.Barrier(2)
    
        def compete(index):
            gate.wait(timeout=5)
            try:
                fill(books[index], str(index))
                return "written"
            except RuntimeError:
                return "conflict"
    
        with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2) as pool:
            results = list(pool.map(compete, range(2)))
>       assert sorted(results) == ["conflict", "written"]

E         
E         At index 0 diff: 'written' != 'conflict'

_____ test_same_token_with_competing_payloads_cannot_claim_both_succeeded ______

tmp_path = PosixPath('/tmp/pytest-of-agent/pytest-1/test_same_token_with_competing0')

    def test_same_token_with_competing_payloads_cannot_claim_both_succeeded(tmp_path):
        path = tmp_path / "book.json"
        books = [Ledger(path, authority="EVALUATION", initial_capital=100), Ledger(path)]
        gate = threading.Barrier(2)
    
        def compete(index):
            gate.wait(timeout=5)
            try:
                fill(books[index], quantity=index + 1)
                return "written"
            except RuntimeError:
                return "conflict"
    
        with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2) as pool:
            outcomes = list(pool.map(compete, range(2)))
>       loser = outcomes.index("conflict")
                ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^
E       ValueError: 'conflict' is not in list

    def test_independent_processes_cannot_both_acknowledge_conflicting_stale_campaigns(tmp_path):
        path, _ = loaded(tmp_path)
        code = '''
    import sys
    from pathlib import Path
    from autonomous_research.runtime import CampaignState
    state = CampaignState.load_or_create(Path(sys.argv[1]), 'ignored')
    print('READY', flush=True)
    sys.stdin.readline()
    state.latest_lesson = sys.argv[2]
    try:
        state.save(Path(sys.argv[1]))
    except RuntimeError:
        print('CONFLICT', flush=True)
    else:
        print('ACKNOWLEDGED', flush=True)
    '''
        procs = [subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, '-c', code, str(path), str(i)],
                                  text=True, stdin=subprocess.PIPE, stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.PIPE)
                 for i in range(2)]
        try:
            for proc in procs:
                assert proc.stdout.readline().strip() == 'READY'
            results = []
            for proc in procs:
                out, err = proc.communicate('go\n', timeout=10)
                assert proc.returncode == 0, err
                results.append(out.strip())
>           assert sorted(results) == ['ACKNOWLEDGED', 'CONFLICT']

tmp_path = PosixPath('/tmp/pytest-of-agent/pytest-1/test_shared_heartbeat_is_seria0')
after_commit = None, first_method = 'heartbeat'

    @pytest.mark.parametrize('after_commit', [None, False, True])
    @pytest.mark.parametrize('first_method', ['heartbeat', 'save'])
    def test_shared_heartbeat_is_serialized_through_persist_and_failure_recovery(tmp_path, after_commit, first_method):
        path, state = loaded(tmp_path)
        state.latest_lesson = 'pending-first-writer'
        entered, release, second_started, second_done = (threading.Event() for _ in range(4))
    
        def publication(target, document):
            if threading.current_thread().name == 'first-heartbeat':
                entered.set()
                assert release.wait(5)
                if after_commit is False:
                    raise OSError('before publication')
                write_json(target, document)
                if after_commit is True:
                    raise OSError('after publication before ACK')
            else:
                write_json(target, document)
    
        def first():
            threading.current_thread().name = 'first-heartbeat'
            try:
                getattr(state, first_method)(path)
            except OSError:
                return 'error'
            return 'ok'
    
        def second():
            second_started.set()
            try:
                state.heartbeat(path)
            finally:
                second_done.set()
    
        with patch.object(runtime, '_write_json', side_effect=publication), \
             patch.object(runtime, 'utc_now', side_effect=(['2026-10-23T00:00:01+00:00', '2026-10-23T00:00:02+00:00'] if first_method == 'heartbeat' else ['2026-10-23T00:00:02+00:00'])):
            with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2) as pool:
                a = pool.submit(first)
                try:
                    assert entered.wait(5)
                    b = pool.submit(second)
                    assert second_started.wait(5)
>                   assert not second_done.wait(.15)
E                   assert not True
E                    +  where True = wait(0.15)

    @pytest.mark.parametrize('after_commit', [None, False, True])
    @pytest.mark.parametrize('first_method', ['heartbeat', 'save'])
    def test_shared_heartbeat_is_serialized_through_persist_and_failure_recovery(tmp_path, after_commit, first_method):
        path, state = loaded(tmp_path)
        state.latest_lesson = 'pending-first-writer'
        entered, release, second_started, second_done = (threading.Event() for _ in range(4))
    
        def publication(target, document):
            if threading.current_thread().name == 'first-heartbeat':
                entered.set()
                assert release.wait(5)
                if after_commit is False:
                    raise OSError('before publication')
                write_json(target, document)
                if after_commit is True:
                    raise OSError('after publication before ACK')
            else:
                write_json(target, document)
    
        def first():
            threading.current_thread().name = 'first-heartbeat'
            try:
                getattr(state, first_method)(path)
            except OSError:
                return 'error'
            return 'ok'
    
        def second():
            second_started.set()
            try:
                state.heartbeat(path)
            finally:
                second_done.set()
    
        with patch.object(runtime, '_write_json', side_effect=publication), \
             patch.object(runtime, 'utc_now', side_effect=(['2026-10-23T00:00:01+00:00', '2026-10-23T00:00:02+00:00'] if first_method == 'heartbeat' else ['2026-10-23T00:00:02+00:00'])):
            with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2) as pool:
                a = pool.submit(first)
                try:
                    assert entered.wait(5)
                    b = pool.submit(second)
                    assert second_started.wait(5)
>                   assert not second_done.wait(.15)
E                   assert not True
E                    +  where True = wait(0.15)
E                    +    where wait = <threading.Event at 0x7f901fadd4c0: set>.wait

    def test_other_process_cannot_publish_while_first_persistence_is_unacknowledged(tmp_path):
        path, _ = loaded(tmp_path)

        code = '''

    from pathlib import Path
    from autonomous_research import runtime
    from quant.state import write_json
    path, mode, done = Path(sys.argv[1]), sys.argv[2], Path(sys.argv[3])
    state = runtime.CampaignState.load_or_create(path, 'ignored')
    state.latest_lesson = mode
    if mode == 'first':
        def paused(target, payload):
            print('IN_PERSISTENCE', flush=True)
            sys.stdin.readline()
            write_json(target, payload)
        runtime._write_json = paused
    else:
        print('STARTING', flush=True)
    try:
        state.save(path)
    except RuntimeError:
        result = 'CONFLICT'
    else:
        result = 'ACKNOWLEDGED'
    if mode == 'second':
        done.write_text(result)
    print(result, flush=True)
    '''

        second = None
        try:
            assert first.stdout.readline().strip() == 'IN_PERSISTENCE'
            second = subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, '-c', code, str(path), 'second', str(done)],
                                      text=True, stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.PIPE)

    @pytest.mark.parametrize("after_write", [False, True])
    @pytest.mark.parametrize("operation", ["begin", "commit", "begin_session", "close_session"])
    def test_write_failure_and_lost_ack_recover_the_same_object(tmp_path, operation, after_write):
        path = tmp_path / "journal.json"
        journal = DeskJournal(path)
        if operation == "commit":
            begin(journal)
        if operation == "close_session":
            journal.begin_session(DAY, {"cash": 100}, {"cash": 100})
        actions = {
            "begin": lambda: begin(journal),
            "commit": lambda: journal.commit("one", "fixture", "BOOKED", DAY, True),
            "begin_session": lambda: journal.begin_session(DAY, {"cash": 100}, {"cash": 100}),
            "close_session": lambda: journal.close_session(DAY),
        }
    
        def fail(path, payload):
            if after_write:
                write_json(path, payload)
            raise OSError("fixture disk/ack failure")
    
        with patch("quant.desk.journal.write_json", side_effect=fail):
            with pytest.raises(OSError):
                actions[operation]()
        # Identical retry must reach the same durable state as uninterrupted use.
        actions[operation]()
        recovered = DeskJournal(path)

    def _atomic_write(path: Path, payload: bytes) -> None:
        path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        temporary = path.parent / f".{path.name}.{os.getpid()}.tmp"

                handle.write(payload)

    def test_index_check_and_append_are_one_exclusive_operation(tmp_path):
        store = EvidenceStore(tmp_path)
        entered, release, second_checked = threading.Event(), threading.Event(), threading.Event()
        original_append = evidence._append_jsonl
        original_read = evidence._read_jsonl_strict
    
        def delayed_append(path, record):
            entered.set()
            assert release.wait(timeout=5)
            return original_append(path, record)
    
        def observed_read(path):
            if threading.current_thread().name == "second-index-writer":
                second_checked.set()
            return original_read(path)
    
        record = {"kind": "raw_object", "sha256": "fixture", "size_bytes": 0}
        with patch.object(evidence, "_append_jsonl", side_effect=delayed_append), \
                patch.object(evidence, "_read_jsonl_strict", side_effect=observed_read):
            with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2) as pool:
                first = pool.submit(store._append_manifest_once, record, "sha256")
                assert entered.wait(timeout=5)
    
                def second():
                    threading.current_thread().name = "second-index-writer"
                    store._append_manifest_once(record, "sha256")

self = ExecutionModel(commission_bps=0.5, half_spread_bps=1.0, impact_bps_at_full_participation=10.0, max_participation=0, adv_lookback_days=20)
panel = <quant.dataplane.panel.PricePanel object at 0x7f901e631f70>
symbol = 'SYNTH', quantity = 1, signal_date = '2026-10-19'
execution_date = '2026-10-20'

    def fill(self, panel: PricePanel, symbol: str, quantity: float, signal_date: str,
             execution_date: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """Model one order. Returns the (possibly capacity-truncated) fill."""
        reference = panel.adjusted(execution_date, symbol, "open")
        capacity = self.adv(panel, symbol, signal_date) * self.max_participation
        requested_notional = abs(quantity) * reference
        truncated = False
        if capacity > 0 and requested_notional > capacity:
            quantity = math.copysign(capacity / reference, quantity)
            requested_notional = capacity
            truncated = True
        participation = (requested_notional / self.adv(panel, symbol, signal_date)
                         if self.adv(panel, symbol, signal_date) > 0 else 0.0)
        impact_bps = self.impact_bps_at_full_participation * math.sqrt(
>           min(participation, 1.0) / self.max_participation) if participation > 0 else 0.0
            ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^
E       ZeroDivisionError: float division by zero

tmp_path = PosixPath('/tmp/pytest-of-agent/pytest-1/test_prepared_binding_is_the_e0')
monkeypatch = <_pytest.monkeypatch.MonkeyPatch object at 0x7f901fad84d0>
mutation = 'append_input', lost_completion_ack = False

    @pytest.mark.parametrize('mutation',['append_input','clear_inputs','experiment_identity'])
    @pytest.mark.parametrize('lost_completion_ack',[False,True])
    def test_prepared_binding_is_the_exact_body_executed_after_caller_mutation(tmp_path,monkeypatch,mutation,lost_completion_ack):
        runner,plan,inputs=fixture();original_plan,original_inputs=deepcopy((plan,inputs))
        entered,release=threading.Event(),threading.Event();write=persistence.write_immutable_json
        def paused(path,payload):
            write(path,payload)
            if str(path).endswith('.prepared.json'):
                entered.set();assert release.wait(5)
            elif lost_completion_ack and str(path).endswith('.completed.json'):
                raise OSError('injected loss of completed receipt ACK')
        with monkeypatch.context() as patch:
            patch.setattr(persistence,'write_immutable_json',paused)
            with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=1) as pool:
                future=pool.submit(runner.execute,plan,inputs,tmp_path)
                try:
                    assert entered.wait(5)
                    if mutation=='append_input':inputs.append(deepcopy(inputs[0]))
                    elif mutation=='clear_inputs':inputs.clear()
                    else:plan['experiment_id']='FOREIGN'
                finally:release.set()
                if lost_completion_ack:
                    with pytest.raises(OSError):future.result()
                else:future.result()
        completed=next(tmp_path.glob('*.completed.json'))
        committed=persistence.read_immutable_json(completed)
        assert committed['experiment_id']==original_plan['experiment_id']
        assert committed['input_hash']==sha256_json(original_inputs)
>       assert len(committed['records'])==len(original_inputs)==1

mutation = 'outcome_dict'

    @pytest.mark.parametrize('mutation', ['outcome_dict', 'outcome_tuple', 'identity', 'required_reference'])
    def test_concurrent_caller_change_after_scan_cannot_enter_export(mutation):
        value = payload()
        expected = core.build_frozen_export_intent(deepcopy(value))
        scanned, release = threading.Event(), threading.Event()
        scan = core._scan_forbidden_outcome_keys
    
        def pause_after_scan(body, path='$'):
            hits = scan(body, path)
            if path == '$':
                scanned.set()
                assert release.wait(5)
            return hits
    
        with patch.object(core, '_scan_forbidden_outcome_keys', side_effect=pause_after_scan):
            with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=1) as pool:
                future = pool.submit(core.build_frozen_export_intent, value)
                try:
                    assert scanned.wait(5)

                        value['documentary_notes']['returns'] = [1.0]
                    elif mutation == 'outcome_tuple':

                    elif mutation == 'identity':
                        value['research_head'] = '9'*40
                    else:
                        del value['validator_artifact_hash']
                finally:
                    release.set()
                result = future.result(timeout=5)

    def write_immutable_json(path: Path, payload: Dict[str, Any]) -> None:
        """Publish complete content once. Concurrent identical writers are idempotent.
    
        A hard link exposes only a fully fsynced file and refuses replacement. The
        caller must use a filesystem supporting atomic hard links within a directory.
        """
        path = Path(path)
        path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        record = {"payload_hash": sha256_json(payload), "payload": deepcopy(payload)}
        fd, temporary = tempfile.mkstemp(prefix=".immutable-", dir=path.parent)
        try:
            with os.fdopen(fd, "w") as stream:
                json.dump(record, stream, sort_keys=True, allow_nan=False)
                stream.flush()
                os.fsync(stream.fileno())
            try:
>               os.link(temporary, path)

tests/test_vertical_immutable_depth.py:24: 
_ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ 

path = PosixPath('/tmp/pytest-of-agent/pytest-1/test_checkpoint_tuple_replay_u0/checkpoint.json')
payload = {'x': (1, 2)}

    def write_immutable_json(path: Path, payload: Dict[str, Any]) -> None:
        """Publish complete content once. Concurrent identical writers are idempotent.
    
        A hard link exposes only a fully fsynced file and refuses replacement. The
        caller must use a filesystem supporting atomic hard links within a directory.
        """
        path = Path(path)
        path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        record = {"payload_hash": sha256_json(payload), "payload": deepcopy(payload)}
        fd, temporary = tempfile.mkstemp(prefix=".immutable-", dir=path.parent)
        try:
            with os.fdopen(fd, "w") as stream:
                json.dump(record, stream, sort_keys=True, allow_nan=False)
                stream.flush()
                os.fsync(stream.fileno())

                os.link(temporary, path)

    def test_registry_deterministic_competing_payloads(monkeypatch):
        registry = state.ImmutableArtifactRegistry('r')
        entered, release, second_started = threading.Event(), threading.Event(), threading.Event()
        original = state.sha256_json

tmp_path = PosixPath('/tmp/pytest-of-agent/pytest-1/test_independent_process_publi1')
second = {'x': 1}

    @pytest.mark.parametrize('second', [{'x': True}, {'x': 1}, {'x': 1.0}])
    def test_independent_process_publishers_have_one_typed_winner_and_no_partial_read(tmp_path, second):
        path = tmp_path / 'checkpoint.json'
        release = tmp_path / 'release'
        procs = []
        try:
            for i, payload in enumerate([{'x': True}, second]):
                ready = tmp_path / f'ready-{i}'
                procs.append(subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, '-c', RACER, str(path), str(ready),
                                               str(release), json.dumps(payload)],
                                              text=True, stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.PIPE))
            limit = time.monotonic() + 10
            while not all((tmp_path / f'ready-{i}').exists() for i in range(2)):
                assert time.monotonic() < limit
                assert all(p.poll() is None for p in procs)
                time.sleep(.005)
            release.write_text('go')
            outputs = []
            for proc in procs:
                out, err = proc.communicate(timeout=15)
                assert proc.returncode == 0, err
                outputs.append(out.strip())
>           assert outputs.count('ACKNOWLEDGED') == (2 if type(second['x']) is bool else 1)

    @pytest.mark.parametrize("fault", ["none", "before_write", "after_write"])
    def test_shared_instance_isolates_mutation_through_publication_and_recovery(tmp_path, fault):
        path = tmp_path / "learning.json"
        store = LearningStore(path)
        entered, release, second_started, second_done = (threading.Event() for _ in range(4))
    
        def publication(path, document):
            if threading.current_thread().name == "first-learner":
                entered.set()
                assert release.wait(timeout=5)
                if fault == "before_write":
                    raise OSError("first publication failed")
                write_json(path, document)
                if fault == "after_write":
                    raise OSError("first acknowledgement lost")
            else:
                write_json(path, document)
    
        def first():
            threading.current_thread().name = "first-learner"
            try:
                return record(store, "first")
            except OSError:
                return "failed"
    
        def second():
            second_started.set()
            try:
                return record(store, "second")
            finally:
                second_done.set()
    
        with patch("quant.learning.store.write_json", side_effect=publication):
            with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2) as pool:
                a = pool.submit(first)
                assert entered.wait(timeout=5)
                b = pool.submit(second)
                try:
                    assert second_started.wait(timeout=5)
>                   assert not second_done.wait(timeout=0.15)
E                   assert not True
E                    +  where True = wait(timeout=0.15)

    def test_independent_processes_with_one_observed_snapshot_cannot_both_publish(tmp_path):
        path = tmp_path / "learning.json"
        code = """

    from pathlib import Path
    from quant.learning.store import LearningStore
    store = LearningStore(Path(sys.argv[1]))
    print('READY', flush=True)
    sys.stdin.readline()
    try:
        store.record_research('fixture', 'FIXTURE', {'ticket_id': sys.argv[2],
                              'lesson': 'synthetic negative', 'outcome': 'REJECTED'})

        print('CONFLICT')

        print('WRITTEN')

                                    stdin=subprocess.PIPE, stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
                                    stderr=subprocess.PIPE, text=True) for index in range(2)]

                assert worker.stdout.readline().strip() == "READY"

_ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ 

name = 'quant.learning.store.os'

    def resolve_name(name):
        """
        Resolve a name to an object.
    
        It is expected that `name` will be a string in one of the following
        formats, where W is shorthand for a valid Python identifier and dot stands
        for a literal period in these pseudo-regexes:
    
        W(.W)*
        W(.W)*:(W(.W)*)?
    
        The first form is intended for backward compatibility only. It assumes that
        some part of the dotted name is a package, and the rest is an object
        somewhere within that package, possibly nested inside other objects.
        Because the place where the package stops and the object hierarchy starts
        can't be inferred by inspection, repeated attempts to import must be done
        with this form.
    
        In the second form, the caller makes the division point clear through the
        provision of a single colon: the dotted name to the left of the colon is a
        package to be imported, and the dotted name to the right is the object
        hierarchy within that package. Only one import is needed in this form. If
        it ends with the colon, then a module object is returned.
    
        The function will return an object (which might be a module), or raise one
        of the following exceptions:
    
        ValueError - if `name` isn't in a recognised format
        ImportError - if an import failed when it shouldn't have
        AttributeError - if a failure occurred when traversing the object hierarchy
                         within the imported package to get to the desired object.
        """
        global _NAME_PATTERN
        if _NAME_PATTERN is None:
            # Lazy import to speedup Python startup time
            import re
            dotted_words = r'(?!\d)(\w+)(\.(?!\d)(\w+))*'
            _NAME_PATTERN = re.compile(f'^(?P<pkg>{dotted_words})'
                                       f'(?P<cln>:(?P<obj>{dotted_words})?)?$',
                                       re.UNICODE)
    
        m = _NAME_PATTERN.match(name)
        if not m:
            raise ValueError(f'invalid format: {name!r}')
        gd = m.groupdict()
        if gd.get('cln'):
            # there is a colon - a one-step import is all that's needed
            mod = importlib.import_module(gd['pkg'])
            parts = gd.get('obj')
            parts = parts.split('.') if parts else []
        else:
            # no colon - have to iterate to find the package boundary
            parts = name.split('.')
            modname = parts.pop(0)
            # first part *must* be a module/package.
            mod = importlib.import_module(modname)
            while parts:
                p = parts[0]
                s = f'{modname}.{p}'
                try:
                    mod = importlib.import_module(s)
                    parts.pop(0)
                    modname = s
                except ImportError:
                    break
        # if we reach this point, mod is the module, already imported, and
        # parts is the list of parts in the object hierarchy to be traversed, or
        # an empty list if just the module is wanted.
        result = mod
        for p in parts:
>           result = getattr(result, p)
                     ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^
E           AttributeError: module 'quant.learning.store' has no attribute 'os'

_ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ 

name = 'quant.learning.store.fcntl'

    def resolve_name(name):
        """
        Resolve a name to an object.
    
        It is expected that `name` will be a string in one of the following
        formats, where W is shorthand for a valid Python identifier and dot stands
        for a literal period in these pseudo-regexes:
    
        W(.W)*
        W(.W)*:(W(.W)*)?
    
        The first form is intended for backward compatibility only. It assumes that
        some part of the dotted name is a package, and the rest is an object
        somewhere within that package, possibly nested inside other objects.
        Because the place where the package stops and the object hierarchy starts
        can't be inferred by inspection, repeated attempts to import must be done
        with this form.
    
        In the second form, the caller makes the division point clear through the
        provision of a single colon: the dotted name to the left of the colon is a
        package to be imported, and the dotted name to the right is the object
        hierarchy within that package. Only one import is needed in this form. If
        it ends with the colon, then a module object is returned.
    
        The function will return an object (which might be a module), or raise one
        of the following exceptions:
    
        ValueError - if `name` isn't in a recognised format
        ImportError - if an import failed when it shouldn't have
        AttributeError - if a failure occurred when traversing the object hierarchy
                         within the imported package to get to the desired object.
        """
        global _NAME_PATTERN
        if _NAME_PATTERN is None:
            # Lazy import to speedup Python startup time
            import re
            dotted_words = r'(?!\d)(\w+)(\.(?!\d)(\w+))*'
            _NAME_PATTERN = re.compile(f'^(?P<pkg>{dotted_words})'
                                       f'(?P<cln>:(?P<obj>{dotted_words})?)?$',
                                       re.UNICODE)
    
        m = _NAME_PATTERN.match(name)
        if not m:
            raise ValueError(f'invalid format: {name!r}')
        gd = m.groupdict()
        if gd.get('cln'):
            # there is a colon - a one-step import is all that's needed
            mod = importlib.import_module(gd['pkg'])
            parts = gd.get('obj')
            parts = parts.split('.') if parts else []
        else:
            # no colon - have to iterate to find the package boundary
            parts = name.split('.')
            modname = parts.pop(0)
            # first part *must* be a module/package.
            mod = importlib.import_module(modname)
            while parts:
                p = parts[0]
                s = f'{modname}.{p}'
                try:
                    mod = importlib.import_module(s)
                    parts.pop(0)
                    modname = s
                except ImportError:
                    break
        # if we reach this point, mod is the module, already imported, and
        # parts is the list of parts in the object hierarchy to be traversed, or
        # an empty list if just the module is wanted.
        result = mod
        for p in parts:
>           result = getattr(result, p)
                     ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^
E           AttributeError: module 'quant.learning.store' has no attribute 'fcntl'

    @pytest.mark.parametrize("target", ["quant.learning.store.os.open",
                                        "quant.learning.store.fcntl.flock", "pathlib.Path.mkdir"])
    def test_lock_setup_failure_restores_prior_learning_before_retry(tmp_path, target):

after_replace = False

    @pytest.mark.parametrize("after_replace", [False, True])
    def test_process_death_releases_lock_and_replays_one_learning_delta(tmp_path, after_replace):
        path = tmp_path / "learning.json"
        LearningStore(path).save()
        code = """
    import os, sys
    from pathlib import Path
    from unittest.mock import patch
    from quant.learning.store import LearningStore
    store = LearningStore(Path(sys.argv[1]))
    replace = os.replace
    def die(source, destination):
        if sys.argv[2] == 'True':
            replace(source, destination)
        os._exit(73)
    with patch('quant.state.os.replace', side_effect=die):
        store.record_research('fixture', 'FIXTURE', {'ticket_id': 'first',
                              'lesson': 'synthetic negative', 'outcome': 'REJECTED'})
    """
        outcome = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-c", code, str(path), str(after_replace)], timeout=10)
        assert outcome.returncode == 73
        store = LearningStore(path)
        assert record(store, "first") is (not after_replace)
>       assert LearningStore(path).processed_research_tickets == {"first"}
               ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^
E       AttributeError: 'LearningStore' object has no attribute 'processed_research_tickets'

tests/test_vertical_negative_memory_depth.py:33: AssertionError
__ test_cp061_caller_mutation_after_scan_cannot_change_projected_truth[ready] __

mutation = 'ready'

    @pytest.mark.parametrize('mutation', ['ready', 'ceiling_outcome', 'remove_blocker', 'support', 'validator'])
    def test_cp061_caller_mutation_after_scan_cannot_change_projected_truth(mutation):
        supplied = inputs()
        expected = projection.build_edge_observability_projection(**deepcopy(supplied))
        original = projection._collect_blockers
        calls = []
    
        def mutate_then_collect(*args, **kwargs):
            calls.append(True)
            if mutation == 'ready':
                supplied['readiness']['status'] = 'READY'
            elif mutation == 'ceiling_outcome':
                supplied['readiness']['claim_ceiling'] = {'returns': [1]}
            elif mutation == 'remove_blocker':

self = <quant.dataplane.panel.PricePanel object at 0x7f901e927c80>
fractions = {'a': inf, 'b': -inf}, symbols = None

    def split(self, fractions: dict[str, float],
              symbols: Iterable[str] | None = None) -> dict[str, Window]:
        """Carve the panel into contiguous named windows by fraction of dates.
    
        The fractions must sum to 1. Windows never overlap, which is what makes
        a desk-reserved holdout enforceable.
        """
        if abs(sum(fractions.values()) - 1.0) > 1e-9:
            raise ValueError("window fractions must sum to 1")
        dates = self.aligned_dates(symbols) if symbols is not None else self.dates
        windows: dict[str, Window] = {}
        cursor = 0
        names = list(fractions)
        for position, name in enumerate(names):
            if position == len(names) - 1:
                stop = len(dates)
            else:
>               stop = cursor + max(1, int(math.floor(len(dates) * fractions[name])))
                                           ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^
E               OverflowError: cannot convert float infinity to integer

    def test_planning_freezes_nested_environment_and_metric_identity(field,direction):
        envelope,qualifications,meta=inputs();original=deepcopy((envelope,qualifications,meta))
        runner=DryRunExperimentRunner();plan=runner.plan(envelope,qualifications,meta)

        if direction=='caller':target=meta[field]
        elif field=='environment':target=plan['frozen_protocol']['environment']
        else:target=plan['plan']['declared_metrics']

        else:target[0]['rule']['geometry']='changed'

_ test_planning_freezes_nested_environment_and_metric_identity[caller-declared_metrics] _

field = 'declared_metrics', direction = 'caller'

    @pytest.mark.parametrize('field',['environment','declared_metrics'])
    @pytest.mark.parametrize('direction',['caller','receipt'])
    def test_planning_freezes_nested_environment_and_metric_identity(field,direction):
        envelope,qualifications,meta=inputs();original=deepcopy((envelope,qualifications,meta))
        runner=DryRunExperimentRunner();plan=runner.plan(envelope,qualifications,meta)
        assert plan['status']=='READY_FOR_REPRODUCIBLE_DRY_RUN'
        before=deepcopy(plan)

    def test_shared_queue_isolates_add_until_publication_or_recovery_finishes(tmp_path, fault):
        path = tmp_path / "queue.json"
        queue = PersistentQueue(path)
        entered, release, second_started, second_done = (threading.Event() for _ in range(4))
    
        def publication(path, document):
            if threading.current_thread().name == "first-queue-writer":
                entered.set()
                assert release.wait(timeout=5)
                if fault == "before_write":
                    raise OSError("first publication failed")
                write_json(path, document)
                if fault == "after_write":
                    raise OSError("first acknowledgement lost")
            else:
                write_json(path, document)
    
        def first():
            threading.current_thread().name = "first-queue-writer"
            try:
                return queue.add(task("first"))
            except OSError:
                return "failed"
    
        def second():
            second_started.set()
            try:
                return queue.add(task("second"))
            finally:
                second_done.set()
    
        with patch("autonomous_research.runtime._write_json", side_effect=publication):
            with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2) as pool:
                a = pool.submit(first)
                assert entered.wait(timeout=5)
                b = pool.submit(second)
                try:
                    assert second_started.wait(timeout=5)
>                   assert not second_done.wait(timeout=0.15)

tmp_path = PosixPath('/tmp/pytest-of-agent/pytest-1/test_independent_processes_can1')

    def test_independent_processes_cannot_both_publish_a_stale_queue(tmp_path):
        path = tmp_path / "queue.json"
        code = """
    import sys
    from pathlib import Path
    from autonomous_research.runtime import PersistentQueue, ResearchTask
    queue = PersistentQueue(Path(sys.argv[1]))
    print('READY', flush=True)
    sys.stdin.readline()
    try:
        queue.add(ResearchTask(sys.argv[2], 'fixture', 1, 'synthetic', 'fixture'))
    except RuntimeError:
        print('CONFLICT')
    else:
        print('WRITTEN')
    """
        workers = [subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, "-c", code, str(path), str(index)],
                                    stdin=subprocess.PIPE, stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
                                    stderr=subprocess.PIPE, text=True) for index in range(2)]
        try:
            for worker in workers:
                assert worker.stdout.readline().strip() == "READY"
            outcomes = []
            for worker in workers:
                stdout, stderr = worker.communicate("go\n", timeout=10)
                assert worker.returncode == 0, stderr
                outcomes.append(stdout.strip())
>           assert sorted(outcomes) == ["CONFLICT", "WRITTEN"]
E           AssertionError: assert ['WRITTEN', 'WRITTEN'] == ['CONFLICT', 'WRITTEN']
E             
E             At index 0 diff: 'WRITTEN' != 'CONFLICT'
E             Use -v to get more diff

monkeypatch = <_pytest.monkeypatch.MonkeyPatch object at 0x7f901fb86630>

    def test_append_serializes_hash_and_sequence(monkeypatch):
        import threading
        from research.research_os import scientific_store
        result = AppendOnlyHashLedger()
        entered, release, second_started = threading.Event(), threading.Event(), threading.Event()
        original = scientific_store.sha256_json
        def held_hash(value):
            if value == {'id': 1}:
                entered.set()
                assert release.wait(5)
            return original(value)
        monkeypatch.setattr(scientific_store, 'sha256_json', held_hash)
        def second():
            second_started.set()
            return result.append('TEST', {'id': 2})
        with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2) as pool:
            first = pool.submit(result.append, 'TEST', {'id': 1})
            try:
                assert entered.wait(5)
                other = pool.submit(second)
                assert second_started.wait(5)
>               assert not other.done()
